# T6 Monitoring

Issue: [#38](https://github.com/Pick-Your-AI/pick-your-ai-research/issues/38) · Workstream: [#34](https://github.com/Pick-Your-AI/pick-your-ai-research/issues/34) · Contract: 1.0.0 · Kinds: `monitoring_stack` · Owners: @PUSHINGSQUARES, @appletechie (issue assignees)
Status: complete research decision candidate for independent receiving review and ordinary delivery. The [current decision and acceptance packet](#current-monitoring-decision-and-acceptance-packet--2026-10-19) reconciles all original criteria and received inputs; it does not claim an operated stack or mark the issue complete. Initial source pass: 2026-10-16; every historical access date, source pin and review scope is retained. Proposed Monitoring rule revision 0.3 does not release a successor contract.

## User decision

Which signals must be watched once the build runs?

In scope: health, quality, latency, cost, usage, safety and data-freshness signals; alert thresholds; privacy of logged data. Out of scope: operating a monitoring stack.

Inputs. The Guardrails record ([#35](guardrails.md)), the Evaluation record ([#36](evaluation.md)) and the Deployment record ([#37](deployment.md)) sit in this folder and are cited here by id: #35 rule ids (`G-R21`) and control names (`trace-content-masking`), #36 rule ids (`E-R18`) and gate ids (`G1` to `G12`, with the meanings in the #36 gate table), #37 step, readiness, rule and evidence ids (`#37 S29`, `R35`, `D-R11`, `D-M13`). Rules those records own are cited, not restated. The #2 (T0) fields that set thresholds are latency target (percentile and value), throughput and concurrency, volume, data classification, residency and budget (amount, currency, period). The original pass did not hold #11, #16, #25 or #28. The separately dated [engine input integration](engine-input-integration.md) reconciles the researched #11 releases with the engine signal names below. The subsequent [#11/#25/#28 receipts](#recorded-engine-framework-and-memory-receiving-decisions-2026-10-18) and the actual [#16 → #38 decision](https://github.com/Pick-Your-AI/pick-your-ai-research/issues/38#issuecomment-6256495996) receive their stated research interfaces. The current packet also qualifies the delivered #2/#35 and existing #36/#37 inputs. Actual selected deployment evidence remains separate. Where a threshold needs a field no record defines yet, the row says so and Open questions names the owner.

### Placement rules used in this record

Mode follows the serving-component decision in [T0](../T0-configuration/deployment-mode.md), merged through #81: user-operated serving is `self_hosted`; provider-operated dedicated or reserved capacity is `managed`; provider-operated shared capacity is `api`. Billing units and customer-owned deployment objects do not determine mode. Hyperscaler on-demand/shared inference is `api`; dedicated endpoints and provisioned throughput are `managed`. Training runs do not set serving mode. Select the actual offering/capacity rather than inferring a mode from the provider name.

- A metric the platform emits about a deployment or account (Azure Monitor, CloudWatch, Cloud Monitoring) is placed in T2, even when the platform also serves API-mode traffic.
- A field returned on each response (headers, usage objects, stop reasons) is placed in T1 and applies wherever that response is received.

The `modes` column indexes potentially eligible signals. Resolve the selected offering, capacity, deployment type, model/version, metric namespace and configuration before applying a row. An API selection can have a deployment object and platform telemetry. A documented optional mechanism that is absent may be skipped with its reason; unknown eligibility or a missing required readback remains unknown, and a known unsupported required operation remains a negative result. A mode label alone neither supplies evidence nor makes a gate pass. The [dated applicability recheck](#selected-offering-applicability-recheck-2026-10-18) records the bounded source basis.

### Alert classes

Each signal lists a proposed operational response class, sometimes conditional on its cause. It is independent of compatibility-rule severity and outcome. The selected notification policy must name the responder, channel, routing, deduplication, timing, acknowledgement/escalation and failed-delivery behavior; this note creates no on-call commitment.

| class | meaning | used for | basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| page | immediate response under the selected incident policy | qualified user-impacting SLO/availability/spend-stop symptoms; confirmed security/privacy events follow #35's incident policy independently | symptom-first service guidance (M-O1, M-O2), with project safety policy kept distinct |
| ticket | handled in working hours | causes and slow burns: saturation, pipeline failures, freshness lag, lifecycle dates, budget fractions | the 3-day burn-rate ticket in the SRE Workbook (M-O3) |
| dashboard | shown, not alerted | diagnostics that explain a page or ticket | M-O2 |
| gate | must return data before a rollout step continues | #37 S34 and R33 (alarms that exist and return data before an update); R24 (dashboards, a forced alert and a telemetry-failure alert exist); R26 (the promotion rule lists a quality metric with data for the canary cohort) | [#37](deployment.md) |
| audit | configuration state, checked at each deploy and on a schedule | content-capture flags, retention settings, sink regions, provider logging switches | this record (P1 to P12 below) |

### Four findings that shape the record

1. No universal LLM quality, safety, refusal or cost threshold is adopted. Every threshold in this record is derived from a T0 target, published with its source, taken from the #36 baseline, or builder-set. The published numbers are platform limits, tool defaults and starting-point guidance: Azure PTU utilisation at or above 100 percent throttles (M-M1); llm-d alert defaults (M-S1); the Bedrock output-tokens-per-second alarm starting point of 80 percent of baseline (M-M11); Google budget defaults of 50, 90 and 100 percent (M-C4); LiteLLM `threshold_crossed` at 85 and 95 percent (M-C22); SRE Workbook burn rates of 14.4, 6 and 1 (M-O3); the AgentCore online-evaluation console sampling default of 10 percent (M-Q7); and Foundry's agent-dashboard investigation levels of 10 seconds and 95 percent (M-Q2). No number in this record is measured.
2. Billing data lags the spend it reports, and most budget alerts only notify. Published delays: Anthropic usage and cost "typically" within 5 minutes (M-A1); Azure cost data typically available within 8 to 24 hours for EA and MCA subscriptions and up to 72 hours for pay-as-you-go (M-A30), with budgets evaluated every 24 hours (M-C3); AWS Budgets updated up to three times a day (M-A2); Google budget notifications multiple times per day (M-C4). No opened page states the OpenAI usage or cost delay (M-H20). Provider-side stops exist at the OpenAI organization and project hard limits (M-A4, M-A28), the Anthropic tier cap and workspace limits (M-A5, M-C12), the Gemini API monthly spend caps (M-A31), Cloudflare spend limits (M-C25) and gateway budgets (M-C5, M-C27); AWS Budgets can run an IAM or SCP action (M-C17); Azure OpenAI has no hard limit (M-R6, M-C21). A timely running estimate (SG56) therefore uses the complete eligible cost-event inventory, with token-class prices where applicable, and is reconciled against billing reports. Missing meters, delayed events, held capacity and in-flight charges prevent a complete-budget pass or an absolute-stop guarantee.
3. In the retained source pass, the OpenTelemetry GenAI conventions have no release. Every document in the `semantic-conventions-genai` repository is Status Development and the changelog shows only an Unreleased section (M-O4); a breaking change has already merged to main: the `gen_ai.client.token.usage` histogram is replaced by per-direction, per-operation histograms (`gen_ai.client.inference.operation.input_tokens` and `.output_tokens`), with new `gen_ai.client.inference.usage.*` counters added alongside (M-O5 and the 374 enhancement fragment, M-N301). #36 G12 asks for "a pinned convention version". For this retained source snapshot, the [actual Evaluation receiving decision](#evaluation-receiving-decision-2026-10-18) accepts this interpretation of G12: pin the instrumentation package version, the `OTEL_SEMCONV_STABILITY_OPT_IN` value where the package has one (the deprecated `opentelemetry-instrumentation-openai-v2`, M-O18; its replacement uses the latest experimental conventions unconditionally, M-N311), the `semantic-conventions-genai` commit read here (`f3c6d0c9bb66`, committed 2026-10-14), and a rename map from builder metric names to `gen_ai.*` names (SG148, M-R36). This records the accepted research interface, not a newly released convention or an observed deployment configuration.
4. Content-capture defaults disagree across tools, so the same build can leak prompts through one exporter and not another. OpenTelemetry GenAI instrumentation captures no content by default (`NO_CONTENT`, M-O6); OpenInference hide flags default to False, so content is captured (M-O7); the OpenAI Agents SDK includes sensitive data by default (M-X1); Cloudflare AI Gateway logs are on by default and can include the prompt and response (M-M2); Anthropic's OpenTelemetry export defaults to metadata only (M-L1). The [OpenLLMetry source follow-up](#post-121-source-reconciliation-openllmetry-trace-content) adds its content-on default and selective workflow/context overrides (F01, checked 2026-10-18). The content-capture audit (SG147/P1) checks every exporter and effective override; a global flag alone does not establish what was exported.

Coverage by mode. API, managed and self-hosted are each covered by at least one provider or platform, one telemetry path and one cost path. Mistral and Cohere were opened but give no usable rows: the Cohere rate-limits page documents no rate-limit response headers (M-N312); the Mistral Known limitations page names only an `X-RateLimit-Remaining` response header, with no reset header or unit (M-N302); the Mistral Admin API usage and limit endpoints are Enterprise-only (M-A10); the Mistral status page returned 403; and the Cohere usage fields are shown in a schema list and JSON examples (M-A11). Open-weight API hosts (Together, Fireworks, Groq, DeepInfra, OpenRouter) and Hugging Face serverless inference were not researched; client-side measurement may supply the corresponding requirements only with an evidenced producer/API/configuration binding; the existence of an OpenTelemetry convention supplies no instrumentation guarantee for an unresearched provider. The current SG166–SG168 host/container source candidates address the single-host source gap without inventing an installed exporter, platform permission or a new llama.cpp signal. Vertex AI Model Monitoring v1 supports feature skew and drift detection for categorical and numerical features on endpoints, and its page does not establish the required exact LLM fine-tune monitoring tuple; that support remains unknown, not an omission-based negative (M-R72).

### Signal catalog

Research question 1. One row per signal, grouped by the component it watches: T1 to T5, the #35 guardrail controls, the #36 online evaluation, the #37 rollout, the monitoring pipeline itself, and the SLO layer that turns signals into pages. The issue's five fields are `signal`, `component`, `unit`, `threshold source` and `privacy notes`; the other columns are defined under Comparison fields. `status` is source checked when an opened page states the signal (name, unit, default) for the named platform, hypothesis otherwise. `threshold source` points to a row of the threshold map (TH1 to TH12) when a #2 target sets it.

#### T1 model or provider (per-response fields and provider-side reports)

No opened page documents a set of remaining-quota response headers for Gemini, Vertex, Bedrock or Cohere; Mistral names only `X-RateLimit-Remaining` (M-N302), so headroom for those comes from platform metrics (SG34, SG42) or builder-side counters.

#### Cost and billing

Billing reports lag and most only notify (M-R4, M-R5), so the fast budget signal is SG56; the rows below reconcile and attribute.

Token-only coverage is a partial estimate, never a complete-budget pass | minimize account/tenant linkage; cost events and exports require their own purpose/access/retention | ticket; page only for an evidenced service-impacting stop | estimated; no live billed amount or complete event coverage observed | #39 L1–L79 and current cost interface; #41 price eligibility; current decision M-D5 |

#### T2 self-hosted engines, GPU and Kubernetes

No engine publishes a healthy range for latency, queue depth or tokens per second. The engine rows take their thresholds from T0 targets; the published values are llm-d alert defaults, TGI's concurrency default, Ollama's queue default, Kubernetes probe defaults and DCGM's SM-activity guidance. Metric names differ per engine (M-R35).

#### T3 retrieval

No opened vector-store page publishes a last-successful-sync or document-age metric. Freshness comes from ingestion-job timestamps, Pinecone LSN lag, Vertex stream-update latency or an application watermark (SG159).

#### #36 online evaluation

Most platforms that score production traffic use a rule (filter and sampling rate) that sends a sample to a judge or classifier, and the score is written back where an alert can read it; Weave Signals and Foundry continuous evaluation (`max_hourly_runs`) differ. This is #36 EV-26 (online trace scoring), handed to this record.

### Percentile availability by source

The table records source mechanisms, not a selected SLO pass. Require the exact operation, unit, statistic, population/window, errors/retries/cancellation, sampling and complete required sources. A matching quantile or histogram may support that scoped comparison; a native/interpolated estimate or engine-only duration cannot silently become an exact whole-workflow bound. SG7 needs an actual instrumented producer even when the convention applies. TH1/TH2 and M-D2 govern.

| source | what it exposes | can evidence a T0 percentile | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Azure OpenAI latency metrics | Minimum, Maximum, Average at PT1M | no (M-R15) | M-M1 |
| Bedrock `InvocationLatency`, `TimeToFirstToken` | CloudWatch statistics; the OTPS page uses p50 | yes for statistics CloudWatch computes; start point differs between two AWS pages | M-M11, M-A8 |
| SageMaker `ModelLatency` | `ModelLatency` with percentile statistics (microseconds); `OverheadLatency` with Average, Sum, Min, Max, Sample Count only | yes for `ModelLatency`; no percentile for `OverheadLatency` | M-M3 |
| Vertex publisher and endpoint latencies | distributions | yes | M-A9 |
| Databricks serving metrics | P50 and P99 within the stated Databricks boundary | candidate for that boundary only; it excludes authentication/rate-limiting and is not whole-client latency | M-M4 |
| Hugging Face Inference Endpoints | p50, p90, p95, p99 | candidate only with selected unit and endpoint/window qualification; the retained source's unstated unit is not guessed from an example | M-M5 |
| vLLM, SGLang, TGI engine metrics | histograms | candidate for the exact engine scope; exact good-event L needs the corresponding boundary across required sources, otherwise a qualified estimate | M-S2, M-S3, M-S4, M-N506 |
| Triton | cumulative duration counters by default; histograms and summaries need flags | mean only by default (M-H4) | M-S6 |
| Pinecone, Azure AI Search, `pg_stat_statements` | sum and count, Average, or min, max, mean and standard deviation | no (M-H11) | M-X2, M-X14, M-X15 |
| OpenTelemetry GenAI client and server histograms | recommended buckets 0.01 to 81.92 s | yes if a boundary sits at L (M-R40) | M-O8 |
| Collector `spanmetrics` connector | default buckets end at 15 s (unit ms) | not above 15 s with the default explicit buckets; set `explicit.buckets` or `histogram: exponential` (M-H10) | M-O27 |

### Threshold map from #2 targets

Research question 2. Targets are requirements, observations are evidence, and derived alarms are explicit project policy. The accepted #2 research proposal now includes latency, throughput, availability, quality and budget inputs; its frozen-v1 mapping and successor representations remain distinct. Detailed retention, freshness, hard-stop and notification policies below are proposed build-owned requirements with no invented v1 pointers. Published defaults stay dated guidance; arithmetic and synthetic cases are labelled; none is a production measurement.

| id | #2 target or input | signals | derivation | numbers | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TH1 | declared latency metric, percentile P and value L | SG7, SG8, SG70–SG72, SG76, SG77, SG81, SG90, SG105–SG107, SG155 as actually applicable | Use the exact operation/stream/load/cache/region/population and unit. Eligible good-event share at L can test the chosen percentile; a mean, averaged quantile, first protocol chunk or different workflow boundary cannot. Error/throttle/cancel and missing-event treatment must be explicit | P/100 is the target fraction; positive denominator required. No 30-day window or numeric latency target is defaulted | #2 latency_targets; #36 G6; M-O3, M-O11; M-D2 |
| TH2 | latency target with the actual platform source | SG26, SG32, SG41, SG43, SG46, SG50, SG52, SG101, SG112 | The percentile table describes source mechanisms, not proven fit. Require exact type/unit/buckets/population and complete path; stage percentiles cannot be summed. Joint per-request durations or justified separate bounds may support a different explicit calculation | Bedrock's 80 percent baseline / 3 of 5 datapoints / two-week anomaly history remain publisher guidance for its stated alarm only | M-M11; #36 G6; M-D2 |
| TH3 | throughput demand, peak concurrency, rate/quota and admission scopes | SG1–SG3, SG24, SG27, SG34, SG42, SG47, SG51, SG73, SG75, SG77, SG158 | Requests/second, requests in flight, queued requests, active sequences and token-rate quotas remain distinct. Compare like scopes/units; any rate × latency estimate states its stability/window assumptions and never replaces actual decoding expansion or measured capacity | PTU 100 percent, retained TGI 128 concurrent requests and Ollama queue 512 are published settings, not universal workload headroom thresholds | #2 W-R6/W-R7; M-M1, M-S12, M-N502; M-U4 |
| TH4 | volume, sampled population and alert window | SG10, SG19, SG28, SG34, SG39, SG53, SG66, SG135, SG138 | Requests per period × eligible per-request quantities estimates usage; expected scored items = request rate × window duration × sampling fraction only with matching units and eligible population. One failure among N eligible requests yields burn (1/N)/(1 − SLO) for 0 < SLO < 1 | Synthetic arithmetic: N=10 and SLO=0.999 gives 100, correcting the retained source's 1,000 claim. Minimum count, meaningful cohorts and alert response are chosen policy, not a published universal cutoff | M-O3; #36 EV-26; M-U7; M-D2 |
| TH5 | exact budget amount, currency, period, scope and required stop behavior | Every selected cost event/resource; SG20, SG21, SG55, SG56, SG91, SG119 and relevant provider/gateway lines | p × amount is a chosen alert threshold; budget amount / actual days is planned budget pace, not observed burn or a forecast. Qualify price/FX/rounding, account/product and reset timezone; never add one-off to recurring cost without an adopted policy. Amount actually allocated to GPU charges / (billable count × eligible per-GPU-hour rate) yields elapsed hours for that fixed allocation, subject to the Cost arithmetic qualification. Distinguish notification, admission enforcement, residual in-flight/held charges and reconciled billing | Google 50/90/100 and LiteLLM 85/95 percent remain tool guidance/defaults. M-C13's UTC first-of-month reset is Claude Enterprise member scope, not evidence for a Claude Platform API workspace reset | #2 budgets; #39 C-R44/C-R45; #41 price eligibility; M-C4, M-C10, M-C22; M-U5; M-D5 |
| TH6 | actual data classification, purpose, content-capture and action policy | SG30, SG36, SG45, SG49, SG54, SG67, SG69, SG92, SG93, SG114, SG147 | Existing classification vocabulary does not itself define every permission. ### Cost arithmetic qualification — 2026-10-18

`SG56` and `TH5` use `budget amount / actual days in the stated period` as a **planned budget pace**. Measured burn rate and a period-end forecast require their own observations and method. Apply this pace only to a budget with a stated duration; this wording adopts no amortisation of a one-off amount.

For `SG91` and `TH5`, an amount allocated to the selected GPU charges divided by `(billable GPU count × eligible rate per GPU-hour)` yields **elapsed hours for a fixed billable allocation**. This linear estimate requires a known, positive billable GPU count and rate in one currency. Currency totals and aggregate GPU-hours have different dimensions. Retain actual instance/node or term meters and whole allocations; any per-GPU conversion needs the selected SKU's GPU count. Apply applicable minima, billing increments and commitments before treating the resulting duration as affordable. A shared build budget cannot be wholly allocated to this line while omitting other charge families. See [#39 U18/U19, integration constraints and C-R28/C-R29](cost-estimate.md#unit-normalisation).

These qualifications supply no new rate, alert fraction, FX/calendar policy, allocation assumption or frozen field. Unknown quantities/rates remain unknown; the complete-coverage, scope and period requirements of [C-R44/C-R45](cost-estimate.md#compatibility-proposals) still govern a budget comparison.

## Privacy requirements for logs

The catalog's privacy cells describe the retained source's field coverage, not a blanket permission or anonymity guarantee. The following project requirements bind the actual selected producer and data path; their complete typed policy inputs are proposed for #40. Missing permission, applicability or layer evidence stays unresolved with the governing policy; a generic classification, redaction Boolean or future field name does not certify it.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Optional content capture defaults off as project policy. Any approved exception is bounded per actual producer/version/entry point, initialization/context override, data class, purpose, action authority, destination and lifecycle; inspect all exporters and existing buffers | every actual telemetry producer, including application/native paths with no separate monitoring product | SG147, SG114, SG54; M-D4 | #35 G-R27/current policy; #25 R5 | M-X17, M-L15; [F01](sibling-reconciliation.md); M-U9 |

## Comparison fields

| field | type | values or unit | evidence requirement |
|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | unique string in its record family | `SG`, `TH`, `P`, `M-R`, `M-H`, `M-D`, `MC` and source families distinguish signals, targets, privacy, mechanisms, hypotheses, policy decisions, cases and evidence | author-defined stable identity; identity is not source evidence |
| `signal` | text | what is watched, in plain words | none |
| `component` | enum plus platform | T1 to T5, #35 control, #36 online evaluation, #37 rollout, monitoring pipeline, SLO layer; the named platform | placement rules under User decision |
| `category` | enum | health, latency, quality, cost, usage, safety, freshness, privacy | none |
| `unit` | text | unit and statistic as published (histogram, gauge, counter, Minimum/Maximum/Average, percentile) | the page that defines the metric |
| `modes` | qualified set | API, managed, self-hosted under #81; actual per-signal eligibility can be narrower or unresolved | selected operator/capacity/operation and source scope, not a provider name or billing unit |
| `collected as` | text | metric, header, response field, log category or API name, spelled as published | the page that names it; Development-status names flagged |
| `threshold source` | enum | T0-derived (a TH row), published (value and page), #36 baseline, builder-set, state (audit signals) | published values need a page; T0-derived values need the TH row |
| `privacy notes` | scoped text | documented data fields and potential identifiers/content, with P1–P12; historical 'none' means no payload named for that observation, not universal anonymity or authority | actual producer/path and eligible source; inherited short labels never waive the complete capture/recipient/lifecycle audit |
| `alert` | conditional response policy over five classes | page, ticket, dashboard, gate, audit; distinct from fixed compatibility severity | exact selected cause/condition, responder/route and evidence; this note's suggestions create no operating commitment |
| `status` | attributed evidence-disposition record | source checked/documented, vendor claim, estimated, hypothesis and separately unverified/observed configuration scope; none silently implies independent verification | dated eligible claim-specific source or exact method/result; preserve original qualifiers and unresolved state |
| `source` | ids | evidence ids (M-A to M-Z families), or sibling record ids | URL, publisher, access date, version or date |
| number label | attributed category | published value/default/guidance, estimated calculation, synthetic example, or actual measurement | this note adds no measurements; scope, unit, date and method distinguish each existing published/estimated value and synthetic case |

## Compatibility proposals

**Source checked** means checked against primary documentation, not independently reproduced.

**Rule interpretation (proposed revision 0.3).** Every retained M-R/M-H row keeps its original fixed severity. The current [common rule envelope](#shared-rule-envelope-and-finite-examples) supplies exact participants, separate applicability/assertion, eligible inputs, typed outcomes, examples and migration. For an applicable row, the assertion is that its stated violation is absent: a qualified counterexample fails; incomplete evidence cannot certify its absence. Source checked still means documented, not independently reproduced or executed. M-H rows retain their historical hypothesis status; a later explicit project decision resolves only the bounded policy named in that row. The accepted [#40 typed terminal/CD-3/CD-13 interfaces](../T0-configuration/component-schema-compatibility.md) and [#41 claim policy](../T0-configuration/catalog-data-sources.md) govern; old [#126](https://github.com/Pick-Your-AI/pick-your-ai-research/pull/126)/[#127](https://github.com/Pick-Your-AI/pick-your-ai-research/pull/127) receipts remain historical. No frozen schema, catalog, ruleset or running evaluator is changed.

Severity and outcome follow the interpretation above; missing eligible evidence or unresolved equal-rank ties require review. Current #2 already proposes availability and quality targets; detailed telemetry/retention/incident policy and qualified legal applicability still need the explicit research bindings below. Frozen v1 is not extended by prose.

Rules owned by sibling records are cited, not restated. #35: G-R12 (an enforcing guardrail wired detect-only or fail-open), G-R20 (Gemini API Unpaid Services with personal data outside the EEA), G-R21 (Covered Models under zero retention), G-R22 (stateful provider features under zero retention), G-R23 (in-region processing with global routing), G-R24 (abuse-monitoring human review), G-R25 (vLLM and TGI usage telemetry), G-R26 (Ollama and llama.cpp network policy), G-R27 (trace content masking), G-R28 (CMEK on Vertex), G-R32 (non-Google models on Vertex under zero retention), G-R33 (CMEK and abuse-monitoring logs). #36: E-R14 (judge routing), E-R16 (region fixed at provisioning), E-R17 (exporter protocol), E-R18 (span drops), E-R19 (retention against trace-derived baselines), E-H5 (sample size). #37: D-R1 (moving aliases), D-R2 (retirement within build life), D-R10 (tier limits), D-R11 (Anthropic spend cap with retries on), D-R14 (Bedrock Legacy base and fine-tuning), D-R15 (Azure fine-tune retirement dates), D-R35 (SageMaker rollout alarms), D-R49 (Kubernetes startup-probe window shorter than the cold start), D-R80 (exporter not in a pipeline), D-R81 (no monitoring before traffic), D-R86 (OpenAI residency outside the US). The delivered #35 current selected-feature retention policy covers the Maps/Search distinction and operational failure-mode requirements. Its exact source/feature exceptions govern; the old open-question entry below is retained as history, not a new absence claim.

### Source checked mechanisms (original pass 2026-10-16; current corrections retain their own dates)

A historically reported Sonnet 5.5 QC pass on 2026-10-16 re-opened every URL behind the signal catalog, the threshold map, the privacy requirements, these rules, the hypotheses and the evidence table. It judged 614 rows: 410 supported, 204 partly supported, 0 unsupported. The corrections are applied.

| id | participants | condition | severity | explanation | suggested correction | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M-R1 | T1 OpenAI; client retry policy; SG4 | a retry loop treats every 429 alike, including `credit_balance_exhausted`, `organization_spend_limit_exceeded`, `project_spend_limit_exceeded` and `organization_usage_limit_exceeded` | warning | OpenAI says "Don't retry quota, billing, or other errors that require you to take action"; retries burn requests and hide a budget outage behind a rate-limit pattern | branch on `error.code` and `error.type`; page on billing and spend codes; retry only 429 rate limits (type `rate_limit_error`, including code `slow_down`) and 503 `server_is_overloaded`, honouring `Retry-After` | M-A3, M-A4 |
| M-R2 | T1 Anthropic; budget alert rules keyed on 429 | a customer-set spend limit is reached | warning | Anthropic returns HTTP 400 `invalid_request_error` beginning "You have reached your specified API usage limits", not a 429, so a 429-only budget alert misses it and a validation handler may treat it as a bad request; Claude Code workspace limits can instead return a 429 with a retry-after header | match the 400 message prefix (and the workspace variant) as a spend stop; set the own limit below the tier cap with an earlier in-process alert | M-A5 |
| M-R3 | selected provider/API usage representation; SG10/SG56 | raw usage fields from different semantics are summed, or overlapping classes are double charged, without qualified normalization | warning | Retained sources define different cache inclusion and reasoning/output relationships; an API/model version and billing class determine the applicable formula. The same token occurrence may appear in both a total and its subfield | keep raw fields, units and source pins; normalize disjoint billable classes once; qualify every other meter independently | M-A17, M-A18, M-A27, M-A15, M-A6, M-Q24; #39; M-D5 |
| M-R4 | T0 budget; provider cost and usage reports; cloud budgets | a real-time budget threshold is read from a cost or usage report | warning | published delays: OpenAI hard-limit enforcement "is not instantaneous"; Anthropic usage and cost typically within 5 minutes; Gemini AI Studio project spend up to around 10 minutes and Cloud Billing cost details typically within a day but sometimes more than 24 hours; AWS Budgets up to three updates a day; Azure cost data typically 8 to 24 hours for EA and MCA subscriptions and up to 72 hours for pay-as-you-go; spend can pass the budget before a report shows it | compute running spend in-process (SG56); use the reports to reconcile | M-A28, M-A1, M-A31, M-A2, M-A30 |
| M-R5 | T0 budget; OpenAI spend alerts; Google Cloud budgets; AWS Budgets | the build relies on a budget or spend alert as its spend stop | warning | OpenAI: "Spend alerts do not enforce a cap"; Google: an alerts-only budget "doesn't automatically cap" usage or spending; AWS: costs can exceed the notification threshold before AWS Budgets notifies; these alerts tell someone, they do not stop requests (this row reads the budget pages that #37 D-H11 left unopened) | add a provider hard limit (OpenAI project limit, Anthropic workspace limit) or a gateway budget that blocks; keep the cloud budget as notification and reconciliation | M-A28, M-C4, M-A2 |
| M-R6 | T0 hard-stop requirement (not a T0 field yet); T2 Azure OpenAI or Foundry | the build needs a provider-enforced stop at the budget | error | Azure budgets: "Resources aren't affected, and your consumption isn't stopped"; the Foundry cost guide says Azure OpenAI "doesn't currently provide this functionality" for hard limits | enforce the stop in a gateway that blocks (LiteLLM with its database, Cloudflare spend limits) or in automation from an action group; keep the Azure budget as a notification | M-C21, M-C3, M-C23, M-C25 |
| M-R7 | exact build budget and provider meter/limit with differing currencies | a comparison or limit conversion uses incompatible monetary scope or an absent/unqualified FX policy | warning | Non-USD is applicability, not itself a violation. The named OpenAI limit is USD; the selected Anthropic report and Azure billing scope retain their source-specific currency. A conversion is a dated estimate with direction, rounding and fee/period conditions | show qualified separate-currency lines until the chosen conversion policy is evidenced; record any conservative margin explicitly rather than inventing one | M-C10, M-A1, M-C3; #39 currency policy; #41 price eligibility |
| M-R8 | T2 LiteLLM proxy without its database; T0 budget | a LiteLLM budget is the spend stop on a deployment with no database | error | LiteLLM says budget limits do not cap anything on a DB-less deployment, so requests continue past the limit | run LiteLLM with its Postgres database before relying on `max_budget` | M-C23 |
| M-R9 | T2 LiteLLM or Portkey; a routed model with no eligible price entry | a gateway budget covers a model the gateway cannot price | warning | LiteLLM's named zero-cost counter concerns a request with usage and an expected nonzero configured rate, excluding free/no-usage cases; the cited page does not establish how every absent price entry is counted. Portkey says a model without pricing does not count toward its provider budget | qualify the actual entry/meter and observe expected charging; a genuinely eligible free rate can be zero. Treat omitted/unpriced coverage as unknown for the complete budget, and use the counter only for its published inclusion scope | M-C2, M-C24, M-C28; M-D5 |
| M-R10 | T2 Databricks Unity Gateway budgets; provisioned throughput or direct endpoint traffic | a Databricks budget is expected to block provisioned-throughput traffic or traffic that bypasses Unity Gateway | error | Databricks: "Provisioned throughput inference is not currently tracked", and "Do not use this feature as a way to ensure an absolute spend cap on final billed amounts" | route all traffic through Unity Gateway; track provisioned throughput from `system.billing.usage`; treat budgets as approximate | M-C27 |

## Evidence

The following is the preserved historical source register: its original sweep reports opening these rows on 2026-10-16, and its `supports` cells retain the recorded quotes. The current claim audit below governs any new use; a historical label cannot promote a community report to publisher authority, establish an exhaustive absence, or certify a current deployment. M-L31 was opened on 2026-10-18 and its cell summarizes the documentation. Families: M-A provider APIs, M-M managed platforms, M-S self-hosted engines and infrastructure, M-O telemetry and alerting standards, M-Q quality and safety monitoring, M-X retrieval, agents and fine-tunes, M-C cost and budgets, M-L logs, privacy and law. M-N is reserved for sources added during QC. Rows with M-N ids were supplied by the Sonnet 5.5 QC pass on 2026-10-16, which re-opened each page. Sibling-record ids (#35 `G-R`, #36 `E-R` and gates, #37 `S`, `R`, `D-`) resolve in those records. Its original interim labels are retained byte-for-byte in the table: **Primary documentation**, **Vendor claim**, **Independent measurement**, **Estimate**. Current #41 claim/material/use eligibility and the explicit audit below supersede any implication that those historical labels alone admit the facts.

## Pricing and capacity

Log, metric and observability costs are priced in [#39](cost-estimate.md), line items L68 to L73 and the W4 add-ons. In-process spend (`SG56`) takes its prices from there.

## Recommendation and rationale

Choose an evidenced observation plan for each actual component and data path. Bind requirements and control authority before collecting; qualify signals before comparing; retain explicit no-data, partial coverage and permitted lifecycle evidence. The current M-D decisions and source audit below complete the research design; selected deployment operation remains outside this issue.

1. Use qualified service symptoms for ordinary paging and the separately declared #35 policy for security/privacy incidents. Each route needs a real responder and delivery policy; this record makes no operational commitment (M-D3).
2. Choose the boundary that matches the target. A source-defined client metric is a candidate in each mode; actual producer/operation/unit/population evidence is required. Native engine, provider, tool, retrieval and whole-agent latencies remain distinct (M-D2).
3. Compute a coverage-labelled running estimate from every applicable qualified cost event, then reconcile against actual scoped reports. Do not use a sampled metric or a token subtotal as an invoice, complete budget pass or hard-stop guarantee (M-D5).
4. Treat content capture, provider payload logging and retention as audited state (P1 to P4); sink region is audited (P5) and ticketed when an observed region leaves the permitted set (SG22, SG37). The defaults disagree, so one audit covers every exporter.
5. Apply the accepted G12 interpretation in finding 3 and the [dated receiving decision](#evaluation-receiving-decision-2026-10-18); smoke-test every metric name after an engine or instrumentation upgrade (M-R29, M-R37). The receipt does not stand in for that actual configuration and readback.

### Required observation functions by actual selection

| mode | signals | gates before traffic |
|---|---|---|
| every actual serving mode | qualify the target's client/operation health, latency, usage/cost and applicable safety/freshness evidence. SG IDs name alternatives/functions, not mandatory products. SG142/143 apply only to actual Collector queues; SG145/149 only to the relevant SDK. Capture, destination and lifecycle policy applies to every producer, including application/native paths | the declared #37 rollout evidence, required alert delivery and privacy/authority controls; no-data never becomes a passed gate |
| API | add the selected provider's headroom and error split (SG1, SG4; SG2, SG5), SG6, SG13, SG19, SG20, SG21, SG22; a gateway stop if a gateway is used | apply the eligible provider-operated gates in the next row |
| API or managed | add SG10, SG56, SG17, SG18 and only the selected offering's eligible platform rows: Azure SG24, SG27 (provisioned only), SG30, SG62; Bedrock SG31, SG34, SG36, SG38, SG37, SG59, SG61; Vertex SG40, SG42, SG45, SG57; SageMaker SG47, SG49; Databricks SG51, SG66; Hugging Face SG52; SG162 only for the evidenced managed cold-start configurations (API failure-pattern coverage remains unverified; window length remains a hypothesis). Keep each row's capacity, model, namespace and configuration restrictions; this list does not make every platform signal eligible in both modes | SG160 where a deployment object exists; SG163 during a split with supported dimensions; SageMaker alarms only for the cited guardrail mechanism (#37 R33, D-R35) |
| self-hosted | actual engine/OS/supervisor/hardware only: source-qualified vLLM/TGI/SGLang/llama/Ollama candidates and SG166–SG168 host/container readback; no shipped Ollama endpoint absence inferred from M-H5. CPU-only paths skip GPU requirements; NVIDIA DCGM is not AMD/Apple evidence. Kubernetes/Ray/llm-d rows apply only when present; SG91 only to actual billable/allocated scope | exact engine/process readiness, credentials, required resource/delivery evidence and data policy; an optional product's absence does not fail an otherwise evidenced path |

Add only for actual component operations: T3 source/record/representation/authorization/freshness and distinct retrieval-quality evidence; T4 actual tool/agent/memory producer and lifecycle; T5 actual job/artifact/serving lifecycle. Direct/keyword/application-owned paths do not require a vector service, agent framework or memory_store kind. Preserve #16's existing bound rag_pipeline gate until explicit versioned actual-path adoption. Online judges are optional unless required by the chosen evaluation protocol; every enabled judge still satisfies its independent controls.

Rationale. The catalog supplies version/scoped candidates and explicit project-defined functions. Hypothesis/unknown entries identify their real evidence gap; a source registration is never an emitted metric. The current packet settles initial policy and ownership without inventing thresholds, permissions, customer measurements or an operated stack.

## Structured artifacts

### Native vLLM metric profile (2026-10-18)

The [vLLM v0.31.0 monitoring proposal](../../catalog/proposals/t6-monitoring-vllm-v0.31.0.json)
adds the native V1 engine metric producer as a `monitoring_stack` candidate. It
uses the same release/source identity as the existing
[inference-engine proposal](../../catalog/proposals/t2-engine-vllm-v0.31.0.json).
The source tag was rechecked as `db9527a46873454610df6dbedf79a36d6bf1a7f6`
[VM-S1]. This identifies the researched release, not the latest release or an
installed binary. Catalog status `active` records this research candidate.

This is a conservative mapping into the existing v1 fields. The finer SG catalog,
remaining enum mappings and existing M-R/M-H clauses keep their present scope;
this addition creates no compatibility rule, signal ID or contract extension.

unavailable series remain unknown rather than measured zero. No universal
threshold or collection-overhead estimate is introduced.

This candidate covers the native producer in the researched self-hosted engine
path. A provider-operated API or managed wrapper needs its own identity and
metric-exposure evidence; the same engine name does not establish this profile.
Monitoring does not select a build's serving mode. The item also supplies no
executable v1 association to a selected inference engine; deployment association
remains an explicit receiving input.

The admitted `signals` are a supported subset, not a complete capability list.
In particular, SG74's KV-cache occupancy is not `gpu_utilization`; this proposal
does not reinterpret memory occupancy as device compute utilization. Other signal
families remain outside this bounded mapping. `billing_class` stays `unknown`,
and `pii_redaction` and `retention_days` are omitted because this source check
does not establish those properties for a selected collection path. Neither
missing field means false, zero retention or permission to collect content.
