## Post-#121 source reconciliation: OpenLLMetry trace content

Refs #118, #35, #38. Source checked 2026-10-18; not independently reproduced. This supplements the existing trace-content-masking handoff.

Traceloop's [Prompts, Completions and Embeddings](https://www.traceloop.com/docs/openllmetry/privacy/traces) documentation says OpenLLMetry records prompts, completions and embeddings in span attributes by default. The documented global control is `TRACELOOP_TRACE_CONTENT=false`; TypeScript/JavaScript also supports `traceContent: false`. The same page documents selective workflow/task controls and an `override_enable_content_tracing` context override, so a global setting alone is not evidence that every emitted span is content-free.

Receiving implication: assess overrides and inspect the selected instrumentation's emitted attributes before accepting content-free telemetry. Disabling this content capture does not establish deletion of previously exported traces, retention limits, authorization, or absence of other sensitive attributes. Those remain #35/#38 decisions and runtime evidence requirements. No SDK/version, exporter or backend was run here; the mutable documentation does not qualify every installed instrumentation tuple. The remaining #118 proposal families and original revision are preserved in the [sibling reconciliation](sibling-reconciliation.md); their receiving acceptance remains pending.

## T4 framework telemetry boundaries (2026-10-18; partial source follow-up)

The [paired receiving review](https://github.com/Pick-Your-AI/pick-your-ai-research/issues/35#issuecomment-6245652021)
requests defaults beyond M-R59's OpenAI Agents SDK scope. The findings below
separate trace enablement, payload capture and other telemetry paths. They do not
establish a single framework-wide `default_telemetry_egress` boolean. Method: read
publisher documentation and pinned source text only; no framework installation,
model request, exporter connection or environment setting was executed. Grade:
primary documentation/source, source checked / unverified.

### LangGraph with LangSmith

The [LangGraph tracing guide](https://docs.langchain.com/langsmith/trace-with-langgraph)
(accessed 2026-10-18, mutable) explicitly enables tracing with `LANGSMITH_TRACING`
and a LangSmith credential; the documented default service region is US, with a
separate endpoint for other regions. Its trace view can include user messages,
tool calls and the final response. This is a tracing integration, not proof that
all LangGraph deployments export data or that their payloads are content-free.

For a reproducible lower-bound implementation check, LangSmith SDK tag `v0.4.1`
resolves to `1101a25abf9db9715e0be86b89a98a6092501a1e`.
Its [Python utils.py](https://github.com/langchain-ai/langsmith-sdk/blob/1101a25abf9db9715e0be86b89a98a6092501a1e/python/langsmith/utils.py)
checks explicit tracing context first, then an existing run tree, then environment
flags; with no context/run/flag it returns false. `get_env_var` checks LANGSMITH
and LANGCHAIN namespaces. An existing run or explicit context can enable tracing
without that one environment variable. This is the earlier integration minimum
already referenced by T4, not the current installed SDK or proof about all later
versions, hosted Agent Server, plugins or unrelated telemetry.

### Google ADK entry points and content

ADK Python tag `v2.12.0` resolves to `38381f65f2bee0f70a89f938ee64e51b34916fa7`.
The pinned [CLI option definitions](https://github.com/google/adk-python/blob/38381f65f2bee0f70a89f938ee64e51b34916fa7/src/google/adk/cli/cli_tools_click.py)
set the FastAPI common options `trace_to_cloud` and `otel_to_cloud` false by default;
the Agent Engine deployment options instead use an unset (`None`) default and
deprecate the former flag. Do not transfer one entry point's default to another.
These option declarations alone do not prove the effective exporter state.

The [Cloud Trace guide](https://adk.dev/integrations/cloud-trace/) (accessed
2026-10-18, mutable) documents explicit CLI/programmatic enablement. Its payload
attributes can contain requests, responses and tool arguments/results. The guide
says Agent Engine deployment with `--otel_to_cloud` sets
`ADK_CAPTURE_MESSAGE_CONTENT_IN_SPANS=false` unless already defined in `.env`;
other targets require explicit configuration. It separately describes the OTel
message-content flag. Pinned [tracing.py](https://github.com/google/adk-python/blob/38381f65f2bee0f70a89f938ee64e51b34916fa7/src/google/adk/telemetry/tracing.py)
likewise warns that some default span attributes can contain PII. Disabling one
content path is not a global no-egress claim. Other CLI metrics, plugins and
provider traffic need their own review; none was exercised here.

### Claude Agent SDK versus the underlying CLI

The [Agent SDK overview](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/overview)
(accessed 2026-10-18) describes a library running the Claude Code binary. The
[CLI monitoring guide](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/monitoring-usage) distinguishes
configured OTel export from Anthropic operational telemetry; distributed tracing
is off by default, and content capture has separate controls. The
[data-usage guide](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/data-usage) documents provider-
and account-dependent operational telemetry defaults: direct Claude API metrics
are default-on. Listed third-party-provider paths default off unless
`CLAUDE_CODE_PROVIDER_MANAGED_BY_HOST` makes their metrics default on; a signed-in
Claude apps gateway credential separately suppresses Anthropic-bound usage
analytics and error reporting. Other traffic exceptions remain, and error
reporting has separate eligibility conditions.
All three are mutable guides, not a pinned SDK/binary execution result. Thus an
OTel-disabled SDK deployment cannot be labeled telemetry-free from this evidence;
pin the actual bundled binary, provider/auth mode and effective settings first.

### Receiving boundary

For #25/#37/#38, record actual SDK and dependency versions, launch entry point,
provider/auth mode, trace context, effective exporter endpoint and payload-capture
settings before asserting an egress default. #35's content-approval policy still
applies even when a vendor enables capture by default. A source declaration is
not observed traffic, and an unset optional exporter is not an air-gap test.
This resolves the scoped source questions above only; actual configuration and
network observation, remaining T4 frameworks, third-party tool rate limits and
receiving-owner acceptance remain open. No new monitoring rule is adopted here.

## Third-party tool throttling: GitHub and Slack — 2026-10-18

This bounded #26 → #38 supplement starts with two operation families already in
[T4's inventory](../T4-agents-integrations/tools-apis.md). It proposes monitoring
inputs, not a new universal retry policy or a change to the frozen integration
rate-limit field. Both sources were opened on 2026-10-18; they are mutable,
undated publisher documentation, source-checked / `unverified` for independent
runtime behavior. No API load test, private workspace read, credential change,
message send or rate-limit bypass was performed.

**GitHub REST (M-TR1).** [The primary guide](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/rate-limits-for-the-rest-api)
identifies primary resource buckets with `x-ratelimit-resource`, limit/remaining/
used counts and reset time in UTC epoch seconds. Limits depend on authentication
and endpoint; the T4 personal-token example is not every installation's budget.
GitHub says response headers take precedence over the rate-limit endpoint and
may vary between responses, so remaining counts need not decrease monotonically.
Primary exhaustion gives 403/429 with remaining zero. Secondary throttling also
gives 403/429 but is identified by the error message; it need not exhaust the
primary bucket, and no secondary-budget status query exists. Follow a supplied
`retry-after` in seconds; with remaining zero, respect the reset timestamp;
otherwise the secondary-limit guidance says to wait at least one minute.
Repeated secondary failures require increasing backoff and a bounded retry count.
These are published values/conditions, not observed tenant limits. A generic 403
alone is insufficient evidence of throttling or permission to retry a denied call.

**Slack Web API (M-TR2).** [The primary guide](https://docs.slack.dev/apis/web-api/rate-limits/)
scopes limits by method, workspace and app. HTTP 429 carries `Retry-After` in
seconds; the same method/workspace restriction applies across that app's tokens.
Other methods or workspaces are not restricted by that particular response, which
does not certify that they have remaining capacity. Method tiers, pagination and
app/install cohort matter; preserve T4's dated B42/B43 history/replies cohort
readback rather than applying one blanket per-minute limit. Exact burst limits
are unpublished. A retry delay is not a rate, billable quantity or guaranteed
recovery time, and rotating tokens is not an acceptable substitute for waiting.

### Proposed observation fields and acceptance examples

| Observation | Type / unit | Evidence and boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Provider, selected operation, resource/method bucket and scope binding | identifiers; no unit | #26 supplies operation and integration identity. Retain only an approved non-secret scope reference; never record token values or request/response content for this signal. |
| Throttle classification and missing evidence | enum-like proposed label | Distinguish documented primary/secondary/method throttle, permission failure and unknown; status alone may be insufficient. Keep original header/error classification evidence privately with appropriate minimization. |
| Response limit/remaining/used, when supplied | nonnegative count with named bucket | Preserve presence separately from zero; absent secondary headroom is unknown, not unlimited. Do not sum incomparable buckets or infer a smooth rate from one response. |
| Retry delay versus reset instant | seconds versus UTC epoch seconds | Preserve source units and observation time. A delay and a timestamp are different quantities; malformed/missing values stay unknown. |
| Attempt count, wait duration and terminal outcome | count, seconds, outcome | Proposed client observations, not provider-exported metrics. Separate retry waiting from execution latency; bound attempts and preserve operation side-effect/authorization requirements before any retry. |

Synthetic acceptance cases, not executed integrations: GitHub primary headroom
above zero plus an explicit secondary-limit message must not pass as healthy;
Slack method A throttling does not prove method B is throttled; a missing retry
header must not become a zero-second permission to retry. Unknown classifications
require review rather than a fabricated successful outcome. Alert thresholds and
retry budgets remain #38 decisions, safe operation/authorization and ambiguous
write outcomes remain #26/#35 inputs, and meter units/prices remain #39 before
versioned #40 adoption. Other tool families, installed SDK retry behavior and
actual tenant observations remain open.

## Current Monitoring decision and acceptance packet — 2026-10-19

This is the complete proposed research disposition of the original #38 questions, authored under the user's delegated project engineering authority. It reconciles the operative catalog, threshold and privacy rows above and the actual received interfaces below. The baseline is signed `8d5acbba07603d87d713acf1972645aab67c60f6`, Monitoring blob `e451277b6b040e533014fd5c54c39166f5896d2d`. Its historical source rows, original dates, native profile and named reviews retain their identities. Review, normal protected delivery, actual post-main validation and the exact issue checklist remain necessary before whole-issue closure.

### Selected approach and alternatives

**Decision: select observation functions for the actual configuration, then choose qualified producers and routes.** Do not require a Collector, an online judge, Kubernetes, a GPU, a vector service, an agent framework or a separate telemetry vendor merely because a build is described as production or self-hosted. A required function still needs a complete evidenced implementation, and every enabled path remains subject to its independent controls. Native client/application instrumentation is a valid candidate; a source-defined name or a declaration alone is not emitted evidence.

| Candidate approach | Deployment/operating responsibility | Useful scope and boundary | Current research choice / source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Native application/client plus actual provider reports | Application owns its instrumentation; each provider owns its stated response/report surface | Exact operation timing/usage/errors and scoped reconciliation; provider reports may omit client stages or lag. No generic SDK support is inferred | Default starting point for a simple API path when requirements can be evidenced; SG1–SG23/SG56 and the original provider sources |
| Direct SDK export to an eligible backend | Application plus chosen backend; no mandatory Collector | A permitted simple route can satisfy the requirement. Every SDK/buffer/recipient still needs its own privacy/delivery evidence | Explicit valid alternative; OTel describes a no-Collector path for small/development deployments (M-U10), without certifying this build |
| OTel Collector plus chosen backend(s) | Operator of each selected Collector and backend is explicit | Receive/process/export pipeline; buffering/filtering do not establish durable analysis storage, whole coverage or pre-capture authorization | Choose when its actual processing/routing requirements are useful; components have their own maturity/configuration (M-U6, M-U10) |
| Prometheus with optional Alertmanager/visualization | User-operated or separately selected service | Numeric time series, rules and routing; host/engine exporters remain separately qualified. It is not exact per-request billing evidence | Candidate for native/self-hosted metric paths; publisher explicitly cautions against treating it as complete billing (M-U11) |
| Langfuse Cloud or actual self-hosted Langfuse | Cloud publisher versus operator's actual containers/stores | LLM observations/evaluation with independent stores, queue and optional model API; a local single-VM example does not establish HA/backups/scale | Distinct alternatives with their own configuration/rights/cost/lifecycle, not interchangeable deployment claims (M-U13; original M-Q3/M-L19/M-L26) |
| Other listed provider/managed observability services | Exact offering, account, regions and data path | Original Datadog, LangSmith, Braintrust, Arize, Weave, gateway and platform entries remain alternatives for their dated source scope | No ranking, performance advantage, current quote, licence eligibility or universal integration claim is supplied by catalog membership |

The `modes` cells describe serving compatibility and responsibility, not the hosting of the monitoring product itself. Mixed builds bind each component's actual operator/capacity. Source-level candidate coverage across API/managed/self-hosted completes the research comparison; installation, credentials, live availability and operational readiness are separate evidence.

### Additional single-host candidates

The pinned node_exporter source supplies a bounded Linux host path outside Kubernetes. Dated Docker/Prometheus guides additionally describe daemon/container metrics and container events (M-U17); their examples are not pinned installations or a grant of host/network access. This addresses the old M-H18 source gap without operating a host agent, granting host-namespace access, or inventing a new llama.cpp SG contract. CPU, memory and filesystem metrics do not prove model capacity, decoding fit or process readiness. Use the selected engine/supervisor's separate health/restart evidence. Other OS families and any container-to-host mapping need their own exact source/configuration qualification.

| id | signal | component | category | unit | modes | collected as | threshold source | privacy notes | alert | status | source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SG166 | Linux host CPU, memory and filesystem observations | actual host and configured node_exporter | usage | `node_cpu_seconds_total`: seconds, counter by CPU/mode; `node_memory_MemAvailable_bytes`/`node_memory_MemTotal_bytes`: byte gauges when present; `node_filesystem_size_bytes`/`node_filesystem_avail_bytes`: byte gauges for the selected mount | self-hosted; actual authorized host path only | pinned source definitions and exact OS/collector/namespace/mount configuration; memory fields can be omitted and filesystem errors suppress capacity samples | selected resource policy; no default percentage or measured model capacity | host/device/mount labels can disclose topology; least necessary host access and bounded label/export policy | dashboard; ticket for the selected resource condition | source checked at commit `37ec35f6981b68c0d7c4191a802d8026064d1866`; no installation or scrape | M-U14 |
| SG167 | Host collector success and duration | each enabled node_exporter collector | health | success gauge 0/1; scrape duration seconds | self-hosted; actual authorized host path only | `node_scrape_collector_success`, `node_scrape_collector_duration_seconds`, exact collector labels/version/configuration | declared delivery/completeness policy; missing disabled/filtered/failed series is not a measured zero | topology/collector names are scoped metadata | ticket or required observation gate | source checked mechanism; no actual collection | M-U14 |
| SG168 | Docker daemon/container observations and lifecycle events | actual selected daemon, cAdvisor and container/event collector | health | daemon metrics keep selected-version units; cAdvisor CPU seconds, memory bytes, network-byte rates and `container_start_time_seconds`; Docker event type and timestamp | self-hosted; selected container path only | official single-host guides and events API/CLI describe candidate mechanisms. Docker daemon metrics do not cover application behavior; start-time changes are diagnostic, not counted restarts. `start`, `die`, `restart`, `oom` and health events need exact identity/window/collection continuity; the last-256-event history is not exhaustive | selected resource/lifecycle/delivery policy, no universal restart threshold | names, labels, host mounts and wider scrape exposure need actual authority and bounded export | ticket or scoped required observation gate | dated source checked; actual version/configuration/capture unverified | M-U17 |

### Build-owned field binding

The minimum original fields remain `signal`, `component`, `unit`, `threshold source` and `privacy notes`. The following dictionary makes their exact operational meaning reviewable. These are proposed normalized research inputs, **not new JSON paths or a wire/schema adoption**. `monitoring_stack` v1 has `spec.signals` (required enum set), optional `pii_redaction`, `retention_days`, `exporter`; selection settings have `sample_rate`, `retention_days`, `credential_ref`. Those broad fields cannot encode or prove the full decisions below. A `pii_redaction: true`, `exporter: none`, one retention integer or `sample_rate: 0` is not a complete privacy, lifecycle or required-observation result.

| Proposed input group | Type, unit and admissible meaning | Evidence / unknown boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| identity and revision | Exact build/selection/role/relationship, source document bytes, rule/policy version and configuration digest; nonblank canonical IDs, not a guessed primary item | Validate whole-build identity and all provided field types before applicability shortcuts, including disabled/unrelated identities. Cross-build/revision/digest mismatch rejects; an ID alone supplies no eligible fact |
| actual producer inventory | Explicit set of application, library, SDK, sidecar, runtime, provider, collector, gateway, judge and notification producer instances, with enabled state and independently qualified completeness | A complete known empty optional inventory can establish absence; omission, missing selection or catalog type cannot. A required function has a separate presence check |
| exact source/operation binding | Producer/package/binary and source revision; launch entry point, API/wire protocol, model/engine/adapter, operation, selected resource/principal, runtime offering and effective configuration | Resolve field availability and source eligibility for that tuple. A standard, advertised integration, SDK range or provider label does not prove installed behavior |
| signal representation | Native name, semantic meaning, counter/gauge/histogram/quantile, exact unit and label dimensions; known conversion formula where needed | Seconds versus milliseconds, counts versus rates, queue requests/items/bytes, active sequences and concurrency stay distinct. Unknown units/types cannot be guessed from labels or examples |
| population and time | Request/event IDs or privacy-preserving bounded linkage, sample/eligible counts as nonnegative integers, UTC observation interval and method, operation start/end, collection/export lag, load/cache/streaming/error/retry/cancel scope | A counter difference or empty series is not a complete cohort. Require positive qualified denominator for rates and stated statistical eligibility; no-data is not zero/pass |
| target/threshold policy | Required metric/statistic, positive latency value with explicit unit, comparison direction, window, minimum count and missing-data response; numeric ranges match the adopted #2/#36 input | Target, vendor default, editable preset, derived alarm and observed statistic are separately attributed. No invented minimum sample count or universal numeric default |
| alert provenance | Exact condition/query/rule revision, response class, route/receiver, evaluation/queue/delivery/acknowledgement timestamps and scoped evidence references | The plan names responsibility; actual receipt is only for its observed route/time. No invented on-call promise, all-route success or future delivery guarantee |
| capture and authority | Per-producer initialization/environment/context settings; permitted data classes/purpose, actual subject/resource/actions/readers and scoped authority/assessment | Default optional content capture off is project policy. Notice, masking, opaque IDs, source availability or credentials alone supplies no permission; actual #35 authority/early-effect controls remain independent |
| destinations and buffers | Complete actual processing/storage/export/notification/judge/queue inventory, each with operator, region/location, data classes, persistent state and effective access rules | A source-side failure or forbidden destination remains a failure despite unrelated unknowns. A Collector cannot authorize or erase an earlier copy |
| lifecycle claim | Exact event/object/revision, required layer set/completeness, policy purpose/floor/ceiling/exception, state per layer, timing/method and evidence/access limits | Distinguish acknowledgement, expiration, current serving exclusion, sampled readback, retained backup, restore reconciliation and completed required-layer disposal. No selected label or aggregate count proves whole erasure |
| cost events and meters | Exact eligible billable event/allocation, quantity/unit, alias identity, dated rate/conditions/currency/effective interval, period/timezone, selected FX/rounding and estimate/actual class | Include model/native modalities, cache/storage/tools/retrieval/reranking/ingest/refresh, judges/telemetry, network and held capacity actually present. Unknown required meter/offset/quantity leaves complete total unresolved |
| source admission | Exact claim/material/use, URL/publisher/access time, immutable version or current effective interval, scope, completeness, authority, conflicts and evidence grade | #41 DS-R1–DS-R5 and #60 govern. Public retrieval, metadata, a self-report or an unresolved request is not permission, exhaustive absence, independent measurement or current deployment fact |

Answer states use the accepted CD-1 distinction among provided, missing, unknown, withheld and explicit not-applicable; no string/Boolean alias or source label silently becomes a known assertion. Preserve the responsible path, state, reason, disputed/assumption attribution and policy. This note selects requirements for the successor review; it does not serialize these conceptual groups into frozen v1.

### Shared rule envelope and finite examples

Every retained M-R row and the six M-D decisions use the [shared rule proposal template](../../templates/rule-proposal.md). Participants are actual `monitoring_stack` selections plus the exact instances that supply the workload, model, deployment, engine, compute, data, agent, tool, guardrail, evaluation or cost role. These descriptive roles do not invent catalog kind names; each selected instance retains its real declared kind and exact operation binding. An application-owned producer needs an explicit implementation binding, not an invented catalog selection. Current frozen pointers stop at the actual catalog `/spec`, selection `/settings` and build objective/selection identities; the exact successor policy/observation paths remain #40-owned.

**Applicability and assertion are separate.** A row's participants/scope establish whether it applies; its assertion checks that the named violation is absent. Whole identity/type preflight comes first. For known eligible applicability, a known qualified violation fails with the fixed severity, even when unrelated operands are unknown. If the assertion is needed but unresolved, retain `unknown`; ineligible evidence does not establish a negative. An explicit eligible not-applicable answer can skip its selected predicate, not waive required presence or every other control.

Use the actual typed CD-1 terminal: unresolved compatibility applicability stays unknown; a known false applicability or eligible explicit not-applicable predicate skips; a provided assertion true/false yields pass/fail after known-true applicability. The terminal's reasons and answer states survive. Its old Boolean/None adapters are historically preserved and cannot carry this policy. Recommendation applicability follows #2's separate non-nag policy; once a need is known, unresolved implementation evidence is not skipped.

Quantification is explicit. One qualified eligible alternative can satisfy an existential observation function. This never excuses each enabled producer's independent privacy, support, cost and lifecycle controls. An ALL pass requires complete qualified scope and every applicable member passing; one qualified counterexample can fail ALL despite unrelated unknown members. An ANY disproof needs complete eligible alternatives. A known empty support query has CD-3's `requirement_empty` behavior, not a vacuous compatibility pass. Required-component/function presence is evaluated separately; a malformed disabled item still rejects during preflight. An entirely inapplicable optional inventory is distinct from an unresolved or missing required inventory.

Evidence ordering is #41's normalization, exact scope, version/effective date, authority and conservative equal-rank numeric resolution with a dispute flag; unresolved equal-rank Boolean ties stay unknown. A documentation-only pass remains documented/unverified. Estimate bounds must match the comparison direction and scope; a known violation is not hidden behind a general unknown summary. No rule below is a released runtime gate, automatic source-rights grant or operational certification.

| Decision / proposed fixed policy | Applicability | Assertion and evidence | Explanation, remediation and affected consumers |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **M-D1 — required observation plan**; error / review | A chosen workload/rollout policy requires the named observation function | The actual implementation/producer/operation and required signal set are bound, eligible and complete; selected observed evidence satisfies the requested readiness level. Documentation can support a documented capability, not an emitted-data assertion | Preserve application/native alternatives and all enabled controls. A known absent required function fails; unresolved inventory/producer stays unknown. #2/#11/#16/#25/#28/#36/#37/#40 consume this distinction |
| **M-D2 — eligible comparison**; error / review | An observation is used to assert a latency, resource, quality, freshness or coverage threshold | Actual identity, units/statistic, complete eligible population/window, required count and method match the target; calculations preserve independent stages and uncertainty | Reject known mean/quantile, count/rate, partial-coverage and first-chunk/TTFT substitutions. Obtain the missing binding or show the statistic as an unqualified diagnostic. #2/#11/#16/#36/#37/#40; source policy #41 |
| **M-D3 — alert readiness**; error / review | A selected deployment policy requires a routed actionable alert or promotion gate | Exact condition/rule/response, route/receiver and required observation evidence exist for that policy, including no-data/failed-delivery behavior and bounded authorization | A generic alert feature/default or one unrelated test does not prove the selected route. Keep service symptoms, safety response, delivery and acknowledgment separate; a selected gate with missing evidence cannot pass. #35/#36/#37/#40 |
| **M-D4 — telemetry policy binding**; error / block | An actual producer may capture, use or disclose data governed by the selected policy | The entire required capture/action/path binding satisfies the actual #35/P1–P12 purpose, authority, processing/storage and independent control conditions before the earliest prohibited effect | Preserve known forbidden capture/export as fail; missing required authority/path evidence blocks the concerned protected flow. This policy does not replace #35's detector/enforcement rules or force content logging. #13/#16/#25/#28/#35/#36/#37/#40; #41 source-use assessment |
| **M-D5 — complete cost comparison**; error / review | A running cost is compared with a required build budget or a claimed spend stop | All actual required events/allocations, quantities, eligible rates/conditions/currency/period and offsets are accounted for with qualified alias handling; the stated comparison/stop claim stays within its evidence | A token subtotal, sampled metric, missing price or delayed report cannot pass a complete budget. A qualified lower bound above the limit may fail even with unrelated unknown costs; an unqualified subtotal with unknown credits is not that lower bound. #2/#39/#40/#41 |
| **M-D6 — lifecycle-completion claim**; error / review | A result claims retention compliance, serving exclusion or whole required-layer completion | The exact claimed event/configuration/object/layer scope and every required completion condition are evidenced at the stated time/method. Whole-policy pass needs complete layer inventory; every qualified known violation remains visible | A setting, tombstone, acknowledgement, sample or backup-expiry schedule does not prove whole erasure. Keep non-serving backups and restore-before-exposure controls explicit; narrow the claim or obtain missing evidence. #13/#16/#28/#35/#36/#37/#40 |

These M-D rows are engineering policy decisions for receiving review. Historical M-R mechanisms retain their fixed severities and default `review` for missing eligible evidence; M-H hypotheses remain unaccepted as vendor/production assertions. Missing-data policy never changes a rule's severity. New dependencies are informational reevaluation relationships to the exact changed producer, source/policy/configuration and receiving target, not an execution-order blocker or a new frozen edge.

The following cases are synthetic reasoning fixtures. They define pass/fail/unknown/skipped behavior without executing a vendor, test stack, user interview, physical workload or deletion operation. Each result concerns only the named decision, and a passing row leaves other controls active.

| Case | Synthetic input / selected claim | Required disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| MC01 | Complete CPU-only single-host plan, eligible native client/engine/OS observations; no Collector, Kubernetes or GPU selected | M-D1 can pass the required functions; those explicitly absent optional mechanisms are not required. CPU/OS evidence is not GPU/model-fit proof |
| MC02 | Required observation function, complete inventory proves no active implementation | M-D1 fail; empty optional-kind quantification cannot hide required absence |
| MC03 | Required implementation declaration exists but producer inventory/completeness is unresolved | M-D1 unknown; a catalog match or selected primary item supplies no complete witness |
| MC04 | A disabled unrelated selection has a malformed/duplicate identity | Reject in whole-build preflight before a no-observation/applicability shortcut |
| MC05 | Mean 40 ms is presented as p95 ≤ 50 ms | M-D2 fail for known incompatible statistic; the two synthetic numbers are not measurements |
| MC06 | First stream chunk is known metadata while the target is first generated token | M-D2 fail for that substitution; absent event semantics instead remains unknown |
| MC07 | Two independent stage p95 values are added and called the whole-request p95 | M-D2 fail. An exact joint request cohort can support a separately qualified end-to-end result, not general percentile additivity |
| MC08 | Required cohort is empty, evaluator paused or only unscored events exist | M-D2 unknown/no-data for the required score; a required gate is not passed. Preserve the known pause or coverage failure separately |
| MC09 | Requests/second compared directly with a concurrent-request ceiling | M-D2 fail for known unit/scope mismatch; a qualified stability estimate is a distinct labelled calculation |
| MC10 | A complete homogeneous histogram includes L for every eligible source, matches the operation/window and meets the target | M-D2 may pass that target only. Missing buckets or a different operation cannot be silently merged |
| MC11 | A declared alert has a source-defined feature but no selected route/recipient observation | M-D3 unknown; no fake on-call acknowledgement or delivery result |
| MC12 | Exact required alert route has bounded qualifying receipt; another optional route is not selected | M-D3 can pass the scoped readiness policy; the receipt gives no future or all-route guarantee |
| MC13 | One known required alert route fails while another route's result is unknown | M-D3 fail with both findings retained; green feature availability does not suppress the failure |
| MC14 | A known prohibited export occurs before an otherwise permitted Collector | M-D4 fail; downstream masking cannot undo the earlier disclosure |
| MC15 | Global content capture is off but another enabled producer/context path is known to emit forbidden content | M-D4 fail. If that other path is merely unknown, required completeness remains unresolved |
| MC16 | No permitted capture/authority evidence for the selected protected path | M-D4 unknown / block; optional capture stays off and no authority is invented |
| MC17 | No governed capture/disclosure path exists under a complete eligible inventory | M-D4 can skip its non-applicable predicate; any independent required monitoring function is still checked |
| MC18 | Partial token cost is below a budget, with selected storage/tool/held-capacity or credits unresolved | M-D5 unknown for complete budget; retain the qualified subtotal and uncovered lines, not zero |
| MC19 | Complete eligible cost inventory in one period/currency is within the declared bound | M-D5 can pass the scoped estimate comparison; this is not an invoice or an absolute-stop guarantee |
| MC20 | A qualified lower cost bound already exceeds the budget | M-D5 fail even when unrelated costs remain unknown; bound qualification must include relevant possible offsets |
| MC21 | 100 synthetic currency units / (2 GPUs × 5 units per GPU-hour) | 10 affordable hours. At those conditions 2 GPUs × 10 hours × 5 = 100 cost units. A zero/unknown divisor is not a free-capacity or infinite-runtime result |
| MC22 | One billed event appears in usage, trace and alias reports | Deduplicate only if exact billing identity/semantics prove the views are the same event. Different actual calls, retries or exports retain their own qualified charges |
| MC23 | Delete logging configuration succeeded, but S3 noncurrent versions or other layers are not evidenced | M-D6 unknown for whole erasure. A known still-retained prohibited copy instead supplies a failure |
| MC24 | Serving exclusion is complete for the current event; an allowed non-serving backup remains pending disposal | Serving-only claim may pass its exact policy. Whole required-layer completion remains pending/unknown or fails its known deadline, never automatically pass |
| MC25 | A currently servable revoked copy is known while another backup layer is unknown | Serving exclusion fails; unrelated unknown layers cannot hide the known violation |
| MC26 | Fully qualified retention floor is later than the allowed ceiling for the same layer/purpose | M-D6 fail for incompatible policy. Missing legal applicability is unknown, not this known conflict or universal legal consent |
| MC27 | Complete event-bound layer evidence satisfies the exact required lifecycle claim | M-D6 may pass that bounded claim; no transfer to another event, configuration, future restore or broader deletion claim |
| MC28 | Old pinned capability source is unchanged; current availability/terms refresh is unavailable | Preserve permitted historical fact. A current-state recommendation remains unqualified where its own evidence is needed; elapsed time creates no invented TTL |

Version/migration: this is the `monitoring-0.3` research policy. A future #40 release must choose exact dictionaries and operation/producer/policy/observation bindings, read old v1 unchanged, preserve original source/result bytes and attribution, and obtain receiving review for the actual adapter. Existing synthetic v1 examples, broad redaction/retention fields, old Boolean adapters and a note-only declaration cannot be presented as implementing it.

Any remaining factual gap is explicitly unknown or a dated hypothesis, with its follow-up owner below; it is not a factual claim of support, absence, performance, permission or completion. #41's current source policy, not a source-register grade alone, determines admissibility for a selected query. The new prose uses short factual paraphrases and exact source links; no vendor table is ingested into a catalog, and no training/display/export right is granted.

**Azure safety eligibility:** SG120–SG122 cover eligible API or managed deployment paths under M-U16/M-U18. A customer-visible Azure deployment object does not force managed mode. SG120 uses the actual metric namespace/dimensions; the separate classic dashboard retains its listed-region limit. SG121 needs the selected API/version and enabled user/indirect-attack annotation behavior; annotate-only results cannot prove blocking. SG122 remains the classic preview feature with the documented region, filter, request-user and customer-storage/access requirements. No mode label, source availability, GUID spelling or configured switch establishes actual observation, lawful capture, authorization or enforcement. Missing selected applicability/configuration stays unknown.
